巧新(1563 TT) Trading Buy

2026、 2027 年營運動能向上，投資建議 Trading Buy。  
投資建議 Trading Buy ：巧新(1563 TT) 努力進行轉型，半導體
新事業於 2H26 逐漸取得進展，且再生鋁出貨占比也明顯拉升，
帶動毛利率明顯改善。2026 年獲利表現回升且 2027 年獲利能
力續揚。惟考量近期汽車零組件族群評價不易拉升，投資建議
Trading Buy，目標價 76 元 (以 2027 年 EPS 及 PE 15 倍評價)。  
3Q26 本業獲利小幅增長，因業外收入挹注，整體獲利顯著季增；
預估 4Q26 獲利相對下降：3Q26 營收 20.16 億元，QoQ+1.83%、
YoY+18.48%。受惠於再生鋁採用比重提升且匯率相對有利，毛
利率由 2Q26 的 24.32%提升至 25.90%。但因運輸成本上漲，營
業費用同步增長，營業利益 QoQ+6.72%、 YoY+211.71%。因業
外認列大筆匯兌利益，稅率表現穩定。稅後純益 2.47 億元，
QoQ+247.56%、 YoY+478.06%， EPS 1.46 元。4Q26 方面，預估
營收 19.21 億元，QoQ-4.70%、 YoY+16.97%。獲利能力方面，
受惠於再生鋁採用率高，預估毛利率提升至 27.54%。因運費相
對下降，預估營業利益 QoQ+15.87%、 YoY+342.49%。因 3Q26
已認列大筆匯兌收益，若以保守角度看待 4Q26 業外貢獻，預估
稅後純益 1.67 億元，QoQ-32.69%、YoY 轉虧為盈，EPS 0.98 元。 
預估 2026 年獲利表現回升，2027 年獲利能力續揚：2026 年方
面，輪圈出貨小幅提升，而半導體相關零組件自 2H26 開始貢獻
初步營收。預估 2026 年營收 77.89 億元，YoY+11.63%。隨著再
生鋁採用率提升且營收規模增長，毛利率有望逐季提升，預估
2026 年毛利率提升至 24.97%，營業利益 YoY+43.13%。若業外
較無匯兌損失影響，預估稅後純益 6.16 億元，YoY+93.56%，
EPS 3.64 元。2027 年方面，根據車廠預示量，鋁圈出貨量將由
2026 年的 60 萬顆提升至 70 萬顆，有助於帶動車用產品營收增
長。而半導體營收占比有機會提升至 5~10%，將提供額外營收
動能。因再生鋁採用比重提升，且半導體產品毛利率相對較高，
2027 年毛利率及獲利能力有望優於 2026 年。預估 2027 年營收
92.77 億元，YoY+19.10%，稅後純益 8.56 億元，YoY+38.99%，
EPS 5.06 元。

  巧新(1563 TT) ESG：  
Environmental 環境保護：(1)鋁下腳料 100%回收，製成再生鋁棒，持續循環
再利用。(2)導入各式節能設備具體節能，已降低溫室氣體排放量。(3)屏東
廠自建太陽能光電。Social 社會責任：(1)獲得 ASI(鋁業管理倡議)績效標準
認證。(2)定期進行人權盡職調查與評估，流程和政策涵蓋重要的人權問題，
保障員工和活動參與者的安全。(3)響應地方政府公益活動。Governance 公
司治理：(1)風險管理依公司程序辦理，內控嚴謹，並設有專職稽核部門定期
查核。(2)將誠信經營納入新人教育訓練課程與人力資源政策中，設立明確
有效之獎懲及申訴機制。(3)每年定期發布 ESG 報告書。

個股報告

6 群益投顧 
  【投資評等說明】 
  評等 定義 
  強力買進(Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間≥ 35%  
  買進(Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間<35%  
  區間操作(Trading Buy) 5%≤首次評等潛在上漲空間<15%

中立(Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等 
預期近期股價將處於盤整 
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】 
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。  
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。 
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」、「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。 
停止推薦情境： 
1. 達目標價。 
2. 雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。 
3. 推薦後股價不漲反跌。

【免責聲明】 
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

## Document 043: 20261030【投研報告】市場快訊_AI現金流壓力拖累科技股，惟產業需求仍強勁

### Page 1

本報告係無償提供，僅供參考之用。本行當盡力提供正確之資訊，所載資料均來自或本諸我們相信之可靠來源，但對其完整性、即時性和正
確性不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決
策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。 
AI 資本支出侵蝕現金流，科技巨頭財報拖累科技股 
                                   2026 年 10 月 30 日 
一、 市場動態 
1. AI 資本支出與現金流疑慮再起，那斯達克領跌：美股 10 月 29 日主要指數收黑，S&P 
500 下跌 0.30%、Nasdaq 下跌 1.38%、道瓊下跌 0.25%、費半下跌 1.42%，費半 ETF
盤後跌幅進一步擴大至逾 3%。市場下跌主要因 Alphabet 財報營收與獲利低於預期、
搜尋廣告業務成長放緩，加上大幅增加資本支出導致單季自由現金流轉負，股價重挫
12.64%，再度引發市場對 AI 投資報酬的疑慮，賣壓亦擴及半導體及軟體類股。其他
盤後公布財報的科技公司表現亦出現雜音，Amazon 資本支出明顯高於預期，近 12
個月自由現金流轉為淨流出，Intel 則因 PC 需求疲弱下修財測，使科技股盤後賣壓進
一步擴大。不過，當日 S&P 500 約 81%成分股上漲，顯示市場賣壓主要集中於 AI 相
關大型科技股，而非整體市場同步走弱。 
2. Fed 按兵不動但兩票主張升息，12 月升息機率仍高：Fed 於 10 月 28 日以 10 比 2 維
持利率於 3.75%～4.00%，Cook 與 Hammack 主張升息 1 碼，期貨市場隱含 12 月
升息機率約 85%。9 月 PCE 年增 3.6%，10 年期美債殖利率在 5%附近震盪。 
3. 中東情勢緊張，油價維持百元以上高檔：布蘭特原油 10 月 28 日升至每桶 115 美元附
近，WTI 亦站上 102 美元。高油價增加通膨與 Fed 升息壓力，也推升 AI 資料中心電
力成本，持續壓抑科技股評價。

二、 大型科技企業財報重點： 
1. 雲端業務全面加速，AI 需求仍未降溫：本週公布財報的大型科技企業中，雲端業務成
長普遍加速。Alphabet 旗下 Google Cloud 營收 307.09 億美元、年增 103%，TPU
系統銷售成為重要動能；Microsoft Azure 年增 48%，下季財測進一步加速至 49～
50%；Amazon AWS 營收 459.8 億美元、年增 39.3%，為近五年最快增速。Intel 資
料中心與 AI 營收亦年增 78%，創 15 年來最強成長。各家均表示算力需求仍高於可用
供給，顯示 AI 基礎建設需求尚未出現降溫跡象。 
2. 資本支出持續攀升，自由現金流表現決定股價分歧：Microsoft 於 10 月 28 日盤後公
布 2027 會計年度第一季財報，營收 922.79 億美元、年增 18.8%，營業利益率達
49.8%，自由現金流亦維持正值，整體財報優於市場預期。相較之下，Alphabet 雲端
業務表現亮眼，但廣告業務成長未達預期，且單季資本支出達 513.55 億美元，導致
自由現金流轉為負 90.65 億美元，股價於 10 月 29 日重挫 12.64%；Amazon 單季資
本支出則達 773 億美元，近 12 個月自由現金流轉為淨流出 424 億美元，加上第四季
營收財測中值低於預期，盤後股價下跌逾 6%。資本支出指引方面，Alphabet 維持 2026
年 1,950 億至 2,050 億美元，Amazon 維持約 2,200 億美元，Microsoft 則因第三方

三、 市場看法：
1. 市場不再單純獎勵 AI 投資，開始要求可驗證的財務報酬：Microsoft 與 Alphabet 同

樣大舉投資 AI，股價反應卻明顯分歧，關鍵在於 AI 投資是否已轉化為獲利與現金流。
我們認為，市場評價標準已由「是否投資 AI」轉向「AI 投資能否帶來足夠的營收成長
與現金流」，具備穩定現金流與明確商業化能力的企業將相對受青睞，而自由現金流持
續為負、高度依賴外部融資的業者則可能面臨評價壓縮。

2. 雲端未認列合約持續擴大，AI 需求尚未出現結構性反轉：Microsoft 商業 RPO 達 6,770
億美元、年增 73%，Oracle RPO 約 6,640 億美元，AWS 與 Google Cloud backlog
亦分別達 5,780 億及 5,610 億美元，四家合計約 2.48 兆美元（詳見圖 3）。這顯示目
前市場疑慮主要在於投資回收期與資金壓力，而非終端需求惡化；只要雲端業者持續
擴充算力，先進製程、伺服器與資料中心供應鏈中期仍具支撐。
3. 資本支出 2027 年仍將成長，台灣 AI 供應鏈持續受惠：四大雲端／平台業者 2026 年

資本支出合計約 7,200～7,400 億美元，且 2027 年仍將增加。元大投顧認為，Google
擴大 TPU 對外銷售及 Microsoft 持續部署 GPU 與自研 Maia 加速器，將帶動伺服器
ODM、散熱及機殼需求，受惠供應鏈包括廣達、奇鋐、雙鴻、健策、富世達及聯發科。
融資面則宜留意 AI 相關企業公司債利差及再融資條件變化。
4. Fed 鷹派立場限制評價空間，短線震盪可能延續：12 月升息預期升溫、通膨與油價維
持高檔，使 10 年期美債殖利率停留在 5%附近，壓抑高評價科技股評價擴張。只要
AI 需求未出現結構性反轉，維持短線審慎，對科技股中期偏正向看法。

四、市場展望：
1. 科技股短線波動較大，中期看好現金流穩健的 AI 受惠企業：本週財報顯示雲端營收加速
成長與未認列合約持續擴大，AI 需求仍強；但資本支出攀升、自由現金流轉弱，加上 Fed
可能於 12 月升息，短線科技股波動恐將延續。中期而言，AI 產業將由全面成長轉向基本
面分化，具技術優勢、財務結構穩健且 AI 投資已能轉化為獲利的企業，如雲端與 AI 軟體
平台領先者，以及先進製程、伺服器、散熱等關鍵供應鏈，風險報酬相對有利；對高度依
賴外部融資、自由現金流持續為負的企業則宜維持審慎，並留意零組件成本上升對 PC 等
消費性電子需求的排擠效應。
2. 美債避險功能仍在，中天期債券風險報酬相對均衡：10 月 29 日科技股下跌時，10 年期

美債殖利率由約 5.05%回落至約 4.99%，顯示美債仍具一定避險效果。惟 Fed 鷹派立場
及油價高檔使短天期公債易受升息預期影響，超長天期公債則面臨財政赤字與期限溢價壓
力，中天期公債風險報酬相對均衡。投資級債中期維持偏正向；高收益債則須留意 AI 相
關發行人自由現金流轉負及利差擴大風險。

油价影响发散、房价抑制渐弱

2026 年 10 月美国 CPI 数据点评   
近期中、美股债市场应重点关注的因素仍是 AI 叙事和能源价格。 
 美国 10 月 CPI 数据因同比指标缺失，对通胀趋势的指示性相对有限，但
环比涨幅超预期说明通胀压力仍然明显。其中，房租和食品价格上涨是
10 月 CPI 涨幅超预期的主要推动力。 
 我们认为，美国 10 月食品价格加速上行，可能在一定程度上受到了油价
上涨的外溢性影响。10 月美国食品 CPI 与柴油价格的比价，已再次接近
“次贷危机”以来的低点。油价可能增加了美国农业生产的成本，进而
向下游的食品价格传导压力。 
 类似的逻辑对更广泛的美国物价上涨可能也有解释力。除食品价格外，
核心 CPI 与油价的比价也处于多年来的较低水平，这种比价效应也对更
广泛的商品、服务价格有所推动。 
 美国 10 月房租上涨可能反映房价周期对通胀的抑制作用已经减弱。一般
而言，美国房价周期领先于房租周期 1 到 2 年，从成屋中位售价的同比
涨幅来看，美国房价周期大约在 2023 年 5 月前后就已触底，随后反弹。
因此，当前美国房租涨幅有所回升是正常现象。 
 尽管美联储加息对美国房价周期产生了一定的抑制作用，但美国房价在
经历疫情后一波显著上涨之后，事实上回调比较有限。通常房地产周期
作为重要的周期驱动因素，对投资周期和经济周期的拐点应该有比较强
的塑造作用，但由于近年来 AI 相关产业投资的带动，且 AI 叙事逻辑在
短期内受到利率影响又比较有限，所以美联储即使大幅度加息，对美国
经济周期的抑制效果截至目前也不够明显。 
 我们仍然认为，AI 叙事和能源价格是近期我国金融市场需要重点关注的
因素(《两种逻辑的碰撞》20261108)，对美国金融市场也是如此。其中
AI 叙事的影响在短期内更直接。在美国 10 月 CPI 数据公布当日，费城
半导体指数受其影响不明显，再次说明利率在短期内不是 AI 叙事的主要
关注点，产业逻辑本身更加重要。如果美股 AI 概念科技股抛售告一段落，
美债、中债近期的交易窗口可能随之结束。 
 反之，如果市场担忧 AI 叙事进一步动摇，即使美国通胀暂时仍在高位，
中期内美债市场也仍有交易机会。 
 我们认为，油价对美国通胀的影响有发散迹象，这会进一步强化民主党
在国会换届后限制美伊冲突的动机。而 在中期选举结果较为悬殊背景下，
特朗普政府对伊朗采取激进策略的可能性不大，油价对美国通胀和金融
市场的影响趋于缓和的概率较高，美伊局势失控的概率有限。 
 风险提示：国内外实体经济出现超预期变化；国内政策超预期收紧；国
际地缘政治紧张局势升级等。

Hiwin Technologies Corp.  | Asia Pacific
3Q26 Beat on Margin; Up-cycle
Continues to Play Out
Signs are pointing to ongoing revenue growth and decent GM
heading into 2027.  Stay OW.
3Q26 earnings beat thanks to better GM: See Results Summary .
Momentum being sustained into 2027: Management guides for 4Q26 sales to
grow QoQ (vs. seasonality of down QoQ) and 2027 to be better than 2026,
supported by strong order momentum and slightly lengthening delivery lead time of

6.5 months for ball screws and 4-6 months for linear guides. That said, Hiwin is not
able to fulfill all of the orders on hand because of the labor supply constraints.
Meanwhile, It holds a positive view on the margin outlook because the revenue mix
should be optimized  amid solid demand, though it has no plan to raise prices again
in the near term.
More sub-system level shipments: Besides the traditional linear guides and ball
screws, Hiwin is increasing its sub-systems offerings integrated with motors, sensors,

etc., for  precise positioning systems in CPO alignment and multiple axis rotary
tables in military, satellite, and material processing applications. These solutions
have gained more and more traction among Japanese and Chinese machine tool
customers
Robotics business one of the key drivers: The robotics segment has been Hiwin's
fastest-growing segment in the past few years and will remain  one of the major
drivers. It has risen from 7% of the revenue mix in 2024 to 10% in 2025 and is on

track for 12% in 2026 and higher in future. First, wafer transport systems are riding
on the strong demand from semiconductor fabs. For humanoid robots, it can supply
various components including ball screws, linear guides, reducers, motors, drivers,
etc.; it has received small volume orders from one major US  customer. Last, the
logistics robot project, originally scheduled for late 2026, will be pushed out
because of spec change.
Stay OW: We remain constructive on Hiwin stock. Near-term sentiment should

Results Summary
Off revenue of NT$8,110mn (+8% QoQ/+35% YoY), GM expanded 2.2ppt QoQ, to 36.3%,
surpassing both our estimate and consensus at 35.2%, driven by the tariff reimbursement
(0.6ppt boost), higher utilization, price hike tailwinds, and more favorable FX (0.5ppt
help).
Opex was well controlled at NT$1,502mn (opex ratio 18.5%), leading to  operating profit of
NT$1,446mn (OPM 17.8%), 19% above our estimate at NT$1,210mn (OPM 15.1%) and 18%

above consensus at NT$1,227mn (OPM 15.2%).
With non-op gains of NT$134mn, NP reached NT$1,230mn (EPS NT$3.48), 23% above both
our estimate and consensus at NT$998mn (EPS NT$2.82) and NT$1,001mn (EPS NT
$2.83), respectively.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Hiwin Technologies Corp. (2049.TW)
Base case, P/E. We think this valuation methodology better captures Hiwin's earnings growth
momentum, as its valuation tends to move with the business cycle. We apply a target multiple
of 38x to our 2027 EPS estimate. We view this multiple as justified by the 47% operating profit
CAGR we project for 2025-28.
Risks to Upside
n Stronger industrial automation demand pickup.

n Market share gains.
n Earlier-than-expected revenue contribution from humanoids.
Risks to Downside
n Weaker industrial automation demand.
n More pricing pressure.
n Market share loss.
n More broad-based impact on the global economy from geopolitical risks.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.

below.

## Document 152: 261110_ubs_inventec

Source PDF: all report pdf/261110_ubs_inventec.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-10
Pages: 15
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

ab 10 November 2026
Global Research
First Read
Inventec
AI Servers into a transition quarter ahead of
2027 ramps
Q326 mixed as GPU server ramps dampened margins
Inventec hosted its Q3 results Tues with sales NT$272.1bn, +1% QoQ broadly in-line
with UBSe/street flat/-2%.   Servers surpassed PCs to reach 51% of Q3 sales, with more
than 50% from rack-level AI GPU servers (B300, HGX, NVL72) and ASIC L6 boards (TPU,
Axion, Graviton), while general servers were constrained by components. Notebook

shipments were 4.9mn in Q3, -9% QoQ/YoY with revenue -5% QoQ to 47% of sales,
while smart devices remained 2%. Mix shift to lower margin GPU servers, higher pass-
through component costs from inflation and overseas facility ramp costs dampened
GM/OpM to 4.2%/1.6% below Street 4.6%/2.0%, dampening EPS to NT$0.71, below
UBSe/Street NT$0.76/NT$0.94. Inventory grew NT$32bn to NT$162bn QoQ, lifting days
from 46 to 57 as end customers require more buffer stock.

Q4 a transition quarter for AI GPU ramps
We lower Q4 from flat to -9% QoQ and below prior street +1% QoQ as GPU servers
slow ahead of the VR200 ramp ramping more in Q127 and notebooks drop a further
low-mid-teens. General server mix is improving on healthy demand from CSP customers
securing better priority component allocation.  The positive mix should improve GM/
OpM to +30bp/+10bp to 4.5%/1.7%.  For the full year, server units are growing 25-30%

YoY and revenue 55% YoY while notebook units are now trending down 6% YoY
although higher ASPs passing on memory are lifting sales +35% YoY.
2027 growth drivers stay solid
We lift 2027E sales from +25% YoY to +29% YoY, above prior street +23% YoY with
growth from general server ramps from its two ASIC CPU programs, ramp of L6 boards
for AMD Helios from Q426, and R200 NVIDIA orders ramping up at higher ASPs
alongside VR200 racks to a top 5 CSP. We keep 2027E GMs/OpMs fairly stable at

4.5%/1.9% with component costs staying elevated with AI capex already projected up
sharply again next year and keeping the supply chain tight. For 2028, we have some
moderation to +20% YoY growth to allow for some moderation to capex from power
availability and funding.  For Inventec, capex is set to lower from US$1bn in 2026 to US
$500mn in 2027, allowing mgmt to fund with debt over additional equity.
Valuation: Maintain Neutral and trim our PT
We reflect component inflation and trim 2026-27E reported EPS from NT$3.50/NT$4.25

to NT$3.40/NT$4.15. We trim our PT from NT$65 to NT$59, also lowering from 15x
2027E to 14x, +1 STD Deviation above its 12x average as it increases its server mix over
low margin NBs. We maintain Neutral balancing growth potentially above street on
leverage to AI, ASIC and general servers but offset by muted margins and valuation
already at the high-end of its peer group at 13x 2027 vs. group trading at 9-13x P/E.

Company Description
Founded in 1975, Inventec Corporation is an electronics contract manufacturer supporting
customers' engineering design, product development, assembly and distribution. It is
primarily engaged in the design and manufacturing of servers, personal computers and
mobile devices. In 1998, it acquired Digital Equipment Corporation's Taiwan assets to expand

its server R&D and manufacturing capabilities, allowing it to become a global top-three server
supplier. Inventec is also a global top-five notebook PC manufacturer. During 9M20, PC
accounted for 52% of its sales; servers 39%; mobile devices 8%; and others 1%.
UBS EPS and Consensus EPS
The UBS EPS is an adjusted diluted EPS metric. It is calculated using the UBS analyst's
interpretation of earnings suitable for valuation purposes divided by the diluted number of

shares. This may differ to the way the consensus EPS metric has been calculated.
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
We value Inventec based on PE multiple methodology. Downside risks for Inventec include: 1)
a lower-than-expected notebook market share recovery; 2) weaker-than-expected growth in
server markets; and 3) unfavourable FX movement. Upside risks for Inventec include: 1) a
higher-than-expected notebook market share recovery; 2) stronger-than-expected growth in

server markets; and 3) favourable FX movement.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

두산에너빌리티 (034020)

EARNINGS REVIEW Company Analysis | 건설/원전 | 2026. 10. 30  
숫자로 증명하는 중

3Q26 Review: 에너빌부문 믹스개선, 두산밥캣 호실적 
두산에너빌리티 3분기 매출액은 4조 4,494억원으로 전년동기대비 14.7% 증가, 컨센
서스에 부합했다. 영업이익은 2,612억원으로 전년동기대비 90.6% 증가, 컨센서스 영
업이익 2,464억원 대비 6.0% 상회하는 호실적을 기록했다. 에너빌리티부문의 원자
력, 가스터빈 매출 증가에 따른 믹스 개선 효과와 두산밥캣의 가격정책 효과에 힘입은 
마진 개선으로 안정적인 실적 기조가 이어지고 있어 긍정적이다. 분기 환평가손실로 
인해 순이익은 여타 수출기업과 유사하게 컨센서스 대비 하회했다. 에너빌부문의 수주
는 누계 10조원으로 목표대비 75%를 채웠으며, 수주 대기중인 안건들을 고려할 때 
당분간 수주 업사이클이 몇년간 지속될 것임이 분명한 시점이다. 
기회가 아닌 경쟁력, 모멘텀이 아닌 수주  
두산에너빌리티에 대해 투자의견 Buy 유지하나, 목표주가를 95,000원으로 하향했다. 
목표 EBITDA는 2035년 EBITDA에 WACC 8%를 적용, 목표 EV/EBITDA는 Global 
Peers인 GE Vernova의 2026년 EV/EBITDA 멀티플에서 자회사 실적 변동성을 고
려해 10% 할인 적용했다. 호실적과 수주 증가에도 GEV의 멀티플 감소와 순차입금 
증가로 인한 NAV 감소를 밸류에이션에 반영한 결과이다. 전일 주가는 한수원의 체코 
원전 시공사 협상 결렬로 인해 7.65% 하락 마감했다. 시공 수주는 아쉽지만 두산에너
빌리티의 체코 원전 기자재 납품에는 전혀 영향이 없으며, 글로벌 전력 수요 증가로 경
제적해자인 대형 가스 터빈 수급 쇼티지는 당분간 지속될 전망임을 고려할 때 전일 주
가는 낙폭 과대로 판단한다. 1) 두산에너빌리티의 가스터빈은 이미 2031년 출하 물량
에 대한 예약 주문이 마무리되고 있으며, 2) 또한 AP1000의 우량한 수주 파이프라인
과, 3) 뉴스케일파워, 테라파워, 롤스로이스, X Energy 등의 SMR 제작을 위한 협업 역
시 지속되고 있다는 점에 주목한다. 전일 기사를 통해 뉴스케일파워 SMR 1호 제작 계
약을 체결했다는 소식을 전했으며, 이제 에너지 트렌지션은 기회가 아닌 회사의 경쟁
력으로, 가스터빈과 원전은 모멘텀이 아니라 수주로 입증되고 있다.  
 Analyst 김세련

## Document 242: [207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 단기 부담에도 컨센서스 부합 기대삼성바이오로직스(207940) 단기 부담에도 컨센서스 부합 기대단기 부담_2a8cdc97

Source PDF: all report pdf/[207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 단기 부담에도 컨센서스 부합 기대삼성바이오로직스(207940) 단기 부담에도 컨센서스 부합 기대단기 부담_2a8cdc97.pdf

(26.10.02) 
1,354,000 
제약업종

◼ 3Q26 컨센서스 부합, 4Q26 성장폭 확대 전망 
◼ 5공장 Ramp-up 지속, 신규 수주 회복 여부에 주목 
◼ 투자의견 매수, 목표주가 2,000,000원 유지

투자의견 매수, 목표주가 2,000,000원 유지 
목표주가는 DCF 방식으로 산출(WACC 8.1%, 영구성장률 3.5% 적용)

3Q26 Preview: 단기 부담에도 컨센서스 부합 전망 
3Q26 매출액 1조 3,380억원(+6.4% yoy, +1.3% qoq), 영업이익 5,890억원(-
7.0% yoy, +0.4% qoq, OPM 44.0%)으로 컨센서스에 부합하는 실적을 전
망함. 3분기 평균 원/달러 환율이 전분기 대비 약 5% 하락하고, 지난 5월 
파업에 따른 생산 차질 영향이 반영되나, 5공장 PPQ 물량의 매출 인식이 
시작되며 yoy, qoq 외형 성장은 이어질 것으로 예상함.  
파업 당시 생산이 지연된 물량은 3Q 중 추가 생산되어 4Q26부터 매출에 반
영될 전망임. 5공장은 다수 프로젝트의 PPQ가 진행 중이며, Ramp-up에 
따라 매출 기여도 점진적으로 확대될 것으로 예상함. 이에 4Q26에는 파
업 이연 물량 반영과 5공장 매출 확대에 따라 실적 성장폭이 확대될 전
망임. 2026년 연간 매출액과 영업이익은 각각 5조 5,310억원(+21.4% 
yoy), 2조 4,780억원(+19.8% yoy, OPM 44.8%)으로 전망함.

신규 수주 회복 기대, 중장기 성장 기반은 확대 중 
관세 관련 불확실성으로 둔화됐던 고객사와의 수주 논의가 재개되고 있음. 
10월 2일 유럽 소재 제약사와 935억원 규모의 CMO 계약을 추가 체결
했으며, 4Q26 신규 수주 회복 여부에 주목함.  
PolyPeptide 인수는 연내 거래 종결을 목표로 진행 중이며, 인수 완료 
시 펩타이드 분야로 CDMO 포트폴리오가 확대될 전망임. 6공장도 연내 
착공을 목표로 준비 중임. 
한편, 노사 리스크는 단기 불확실성 요인임. 노조는 10/26부터 3주간 2
차 총파업을 예고함. 1차 대비 예정된 파업 기간이 길어진 만큼 실제 파
업 진행 시 생산 차질 규모가 주요 변수임. 다만 파업 개시 전까지 협상 
가능성이 열려 있어, 향후 노사 협상 진행 경과를 확인할 필요가 있음.

(단위: 십억원, %)

## Document 254: [329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 우려 감안해도 충분히 매력적HD현대중공업(329180) 우려 감안해도 충분히 매력적우려 감안해도 충분히 매력적 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 우려 감안해도 충분히 매력적HD현대중공업(329180) 우려 감안해도 충분히 매력적우려 감안해도 충분히 매력적 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-14
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

HD 현대중공업 (329180/KS) 
우려 감안해도 충분히 매력적 
2026-10-14

조선/방산. 한승한, R.A 고서영 / shane.han@sks.co.kr / 3773-9992

매수(유지) 
목표주가: 820,000 원(하향) 
현재주가: 366,500 원 
상승여력: 123.7%

Signal: 3Q26, 조업일수 및 환율 영향으로 시장 예상치 하회 예상 
Key: 4Q26 부터 본격적인 발전엔진 및 FDC 계약 수주 모멘텀 기대 
Step: 상선 우려 감안해도 4 행정 엔진&FDC 으로 주가 상승여력 
충분. 매수

3Q26 Preview: 컨센서스 하회하는 실적 기록 예상 
3Q26 연결 매출액 6조 464억원(+36.9% YoY, -4.5% QoQ), 영업이익 9,230억
원(+65.6% YoY, -11.2% QoQ)으로, 시장 예상치(1조 70억원)를 하회하는 실적 기
록할 것으로 추정한다. 이전 분기 대비 중대재해 및 하계휴가, 그리고 추석 연휴에 따른 
조업일수 감소 효과와 더불어 큰 폭의 환율 하락 영향(약 500 억원) 때문이다. 홍범도
함 화재 및 임단협 관련 일회성 비용은 이번 분기가 아닌 4Q 에 반영될 것으로 예상한
다. 해양 부문에서 트리온 FPU 프로젝트 C/O 에 따른 일부 환입 발생한 것으로 추정
되며, 해양 도크에서 컨테이너선 선체 블록 일부 건조로 고정비 감소효과 일부 상쇄 중
이다. 다만 올해 연내 혹은 내년 초 추가 해양 수주 없을 시 고정비 상승 우려 확대될 
전망이다. 동사 올해 상선 수주 목표(114.7 억불)를 이미 초과하는 수주 실적 기록한 상
황이며, 고마진 선종인 LNGC 및 VLGC/VLAC 중심으로 수주했기 때문에 ‘28~’29
년 구간 경쟁사 대비 뚜렷한 선종 믹스 효과를 기대한다.

투자의견 ‘매수’ 유지, 목표주가 82 만원으로 하향 
HD 현대중공업에 대한 투자의견 ‘매수’를 유지하고, 목표주가는 기존 97 만원에서 82
만원으로 하향한다. 중국의 공격적인 캐파 증설 지속과 더불어 중국 대형 조선사들의 
글로벌 선주가 발주한 LNGC 물량 및 원가 경쟁력 확대에 따른 상선 부문의 멀티플 
조정이 불가피하다는 판단이다. 하지만 상선 부문의 우려를 감안하더라도 상선 사이클
과 무관한 육상 발전 엔진 및 FDC에 대한 글로벌 하이퍼스케일러들의 수요 확대는 지
속되고 있는 중으로 파악된다. 올해 4 분기부터 4 행정 중속엔진&FDC 부문의 본격적
인 수주를 통해 중장기적 신성장 동력 확보할 전망이며, 해당 모멘텀에 따른 주가 상승
여력 충분히 남아있다는 판단으로, 조선주 최선호주(Top-Pick)를 유지한다.

ASIA MONTHLY REPORT  October 2026, No.307

The Japan Research Institute, Limited  
Economics Department

1 
Topics  Loss‑making firms are on the rise in China's growth sectors

In China, the number of companies operating in the red is increasing. And the rise is particularly striking in growth 
sectors. One factor behind the increase is the intense competition among firms, which has led to a situation in which 
only the strongest thrive. Another is that many next‑generation growth companies, despite being viewed as having great 
potential, are losing money.

■ Loss‑making firms are on the rise in growth sectors 
In recent years, the number of companies in China's 
growth sectors that are operating in the red has 
increased markedly. The share of loss-making firms in 
all listed firms (below, "loss‑making‑firm ratio") rose 
sharply from 9.6% in 2019 to 26.9% in 2025. Looking 
at the industry distribution of loss‑making firms, from 
2019 to 2022, the ratio increased mainly in 
non‑manufacturing sectors such as real estate and 
accommodation and eating/drinking services, 
reflecting the property market downturn and strict 
stay‑at‑home measures implemented to curb the spread 
of COVID‑19. Later, from 2022 to 2025, the 
loss‑making-firm ratio surged in 
information/communications equipment and 
electronic parts/devices; general, production, and 
commercial machinery; electrical machinery; and 
chemicals. These four sectors alone accounted for 
around 60% of the overall increase. 
They are the principal growth sectors in Chinese 
manufacturing. This is evidenced by the fact that the 
growth rates of value added in all four sectors exceed 
the average for Chinese manufacturing. Many of the 
high‑tech manufacturing industries that the Chinese 
government has prioritized for development are 
included in these sectors. For example, 5G base 
stations and semiconductors fall under 
information/communications equipment and 
electronic parts/devices. And robots fall under general, 
production, and commercial machinery. Meanwhile, 
solar cells and batteries fall under electrical machinery, 
and biopharmaceuticals and organic chemical 
materials fall under chemicals.  
Looking at recent developments in the 
manufacturing of information/communications 
equipment and electronic parts/devices, the global 
communications standard began shifting from 4G 
(LTE) to 5G services in around 2019, and ChatGPT was released in November 2022. These developments 
have provided tailwinds for the growth of the sector. Recently, as it has become clear that ordinary 
consumers and companies can make use of AI services on a daily basis, demand for AI‑related devices and 
semiconductors, as well as data centers, has soared. In this environment, many firms are focusing on 
strengthening development, manufacturing, and sales, and the sector's value added has continued to 
increase, rising from 780 billion yuan in 2022 to 910 billion yuan in 2025. Yet over the same period, the 
number of loss‑making firms increased from 113 to 198, and the loss‑making‑firm ratio jumped from 17.2% 
to 30.1%. 
■ Competition among companies means only the strongest thrive 
One reason loss‑making firms are increasing despite operating in growth sectors is the intense 
competition among companies. The fight for market share has led to overheated investment competition

### Page 3

ASIA MONTHLY REPORT  October 2026, No.307

The Japan Research Institute, Limited  
Economics Department

2 
and price competition. In information/communications 
equipment and electronic parts/devices manufacturing, 
the rate of growth of non-current assets from 2022 to 
2025 (+35.4%) far exceeded the growth rate of value 
added (+16.9%). This indicates that supply capacity is 
expanding faster than demand. In addition, the 
consumer price index (CPI) increased by just 0.4% 
over the same period. Service prices rose 2.2%, 
reflecting wage increases, while goods prices fell 0.7% 
due to excess supply in manufacturing. 
Information/communications equipment prices 
decreased by 3.5%, larger than the drop for goods 
overall. 
As the selection of the fittest through competition 
among companies has progressed, profits have become 
concentrated at a small number of highly competitive 
firms. The share of profits earned by the top 1% of 
firms in information/communications equipment and electronic parts/devices manufacturing rose sharply 
from 24.0% in 2022 to 43.3% in 2025. Leading companies are simultaneously establishing positions as 
both "domestic champions" and "global champions."  
■ Next‑generation growth companies are viewed as having great potential even though they 
are losing money 
However, loss‑making firms cannot simply be lumped together and labeled as losers. Globally, investors 
have shown increasing interest recently in companies with high growth potential even if they are operating 
in the red, and China has a considerable number of such firms. 
In information/communications equipment and 
electronic parts/devices manufacturing, one out of 
every two loss‑making firms is a company attracting 
strong investor interest. The Shanghai Composite 
Index rose 28.5% from 3,089 points at the beginning of 
2023 to 3,969 points at the end of 2025. 20 of the 198 
loss‑making firms in 2025 were not yet listed in 2023, 
and if these are excluded, there were 178 companies 
operating in the red as of 2025. Of these 178 firms, 96 
(53.9%) saw stock‑price increases over the period 
2023-2025 that exceeded the rise in the Shanghai 
Composite Index.  
One characteristic of these 96 companies with strong 
relative stock‑price performance is their active 
investment in R&D and intangible assets. Their 
average R&D intensity (R&D investment / sales) in 
2022 was 9.6%, and their ratio of intangible assets to 
value added stood at 60.5%. Intangible assets include 
computer software, patents, copyrights, and 
relationships with customers and suppliers. In contrast, 
the 82 loss‑making firms with weak relative 
stock‑price performance had an R&D intensity of only 
6.5% and an intangible‑asset‑to‑value‑added ratio of just 38.9%. 
As the above has shown, the increase in loss‑making firms in China's growth sectors is notable, and the 
factors behind it include not only the selection of the fittest as a result of competition among companies but 
also the emergence of next‑generation growth firms that are viewed as having great potential. 
  (Shinichi Seki)

3 
Topics  Climate change heightens the risk of growth stagnation in Asia

Climate change could elevate the risk of economic growth flatlining in the ASEAN zone and India by causing labor 
productivity growth and capital accumulation to stall. To mitigate or avert such a scenario, establishing a business 
environment and institutions that promote "adaptation investment" by both the public and private sectors is an urgent 
task.

■ Heavy‑rain and flood damage is becoming increasingly serious in Asia 
Huge downpours and floods 
resulting in human casualties have 
been a frequent occurrence in Asia in 
the past several years. Still fresh in the 
memory is the debris flow that 
occurred in August this year near the 
border between Nepal and China, 
which as of the time of writing has 
been recognized as a major disaster 
with thousands dead or missing. And 
in November of last year, Southeast 
Asia and South Asia saw 
record‑breaking torrential rains, 
resulting in numerous casualties, 
particularly in Indonesia and Sri 
Lanka. 
One factor that heightens the risks 
of such floods and heavy rains is 
climate change. As temperatures rise, 
the amount of water vapor in the 
atmosphere increases. Therefore, 
global warming could intensify 
torrential rainfall. Although such risk 
is shared across Asia, the way its 
effects are felt differs by region. 
Within Asia, the ASEAN zone and 
India are heavily affected by 
monsoons (seasonal winds), with 
many areas experiencing concentrated 
rainfall during the wet season. In such 
areas, warming can further intensify 
monsoon‑driven heavy rains, 
increasing the risks of floods and 
landslides.  
■ Climate change will delay the escape from the "middle‑income trap" 
Climate change not only results in human casualties by way of natural disasters, but may also undermine 
the long‑term economic growth potential of ASEAN and India through various channels. The Asian 
Development Bank (ADB) [2024] conducted a quantitative analysis of the extent to which climate change 
will weigh on Asian economies over the long term. Based on the IPCC's "very high GHG emissions" 
scenario (SSP5‑8.5), the analysis assumes a 4.7°C rise in the Earth's average temperature by 2100 relative 
to pre-industrial levels, and estimates the difference in real GDP between that and a hypothetical scenario 
without climate change. The results illustrate the long‑term impact on economic growth through declines 
in labor productivity, stagnation in capital accumulation, and downward pressure on consumption due to 
sea‑level rise, floods, heat waves, and other phenomena caused by climate change. 
An examination of the results reveals that in every country/territory, the pushdown effect on real GDP 
relative to the no‑climate‑change scenario expands over time, with 2035, 2050, and 2070 each seeing 
greater impact. The pushdown effect for ASEAN and India is −15.0% to −8.8% in 2050 and −30.2% to

4 
−18.1% in 2070, larger than the effects for China (2050: −4.8%, 2070: −10.3%) and Central Asian countries 
(2050: −1.7%, 2070: −3.8%). Within Asia, the ASEAN zone and India can be considered regions where the 
risk of economic stagnation due to climate change is relatively large.  
This may make it even more difficult for ASEAN countries and India to escape the "middle‑income trap." 
Although their income levels have risen, they still remain far from high‑income status. Indonesia, Malaysia, 
the Philippines, and Thailand became middle‑income countries in the 1990s or earlier, but have still not 
been able to join the groups of high‑income countries. Furthermore, Vietnam and India, which became 
middle‑income countries in the 2000s or later, have yet to enter a high‑growth trajectory like China once 
did. Climate change, by causing labor productivity growth to stall amid rising temperatures and companies 
to curtail investment due to more frequent disasters, may lead to income growth in the ASEAN zone and 
India being delayed further.  
■ Improving the environment to accelerate "adaptation investment" will be important 
To alleviate the pushdown effect of climate change on growth, expansion of "adaptation investment" by 
the public and private sectors is essential. Specifically, this could include improving working environments 
and promoting measures to lighten workloads and reduce headcounts in order to offset declines in labor 
productivity caused by rising temperatures, as well as developing infrastructure and strengthening facilities 
to prevent damage to production equipment from sea‑level rise and floods. However, ASEAN and India 
cannot be said to have sufficiently 
developed the foundations for public 
and private promotion of such 
adaptation investment.  
As part of its Global Adaptation 
Initiative (ND‑GAIN), the University of 
Notre Dame (U.S.) evaluates countries' 
ability to use investment to achieve 
climate‑change adaptation in the form of 
a "readiness" score with three 
components: economic readiness, 
governance readiness, and social 
readiness. Respectively, these 
components assess 1) whether a 
business environment conducive to 
adaptation investment by private-sector 
companies has been put in place, 2) 
whether the effectiveness of institutions

and policies to support private-sector adaptation investment has been secured, and 3) whether tangible and 
intangible social infrastructure that promotes investment has been established. 
Looking at readiness in 2024, ASEAN countries and India generally rank in the middle to lower tiers 
globally, with a large gap between them and developed countries. Notably, the Philippines, India, and 
Thailand fall outside the top 100, indicating weak momentum for adaptation investment. Breaking down 
the scores, problems are especially evident in the economic and governance categories. India and Thailand 
have particularly low economic readiness, ranking 113th and 124th, respectively, and five countries 
(Malaysia not included) fall outside the top 100 for governance readiness. Given this situation, to accelerate 
private‑sector adaptation investment, India and Thailand in particular need to move swiftly to improve the 
financial environment to make it easier to procure necessary funds domestically and internationally, relax 
regulations to encourage the launch of new businesses, and reform labor markets to enable the smooth 
securing of human resources. In addition, for Asia as a whole, it will also be important to boost confidence 
in the institutional factors that enhance application of investment for adaptation by increasing policy 
stability, ensuring the effectiveness of legal systems, and curbing corruption.

(Tomohiro Hosoi)

### Page 4

Monthly Economics
Sector Analysis
Personal Consumption Expenditures (PCE)
• We've lifted our Q3 expectation for real PCE on incoming data and a slightly firmer consumer
backdrop in the wake of recent revisions.
Consumer spending looks firmer in the third quarter, and we now expect real PCE to rise at a 3.4%
annualized clip. This upward revision reflects recent data, with August consumption coming in firm
with real spending rising by the fastest monthly clip in nearly two years, but also reflects a slightly

firmer consumer backdrop in the wake of the BEA's annual revisions released late September. Although
revisions did not materially alter spending, they lifted the level of household income in recent years,
which also boosted the personal saving rate. The trajectory of real income growth remains weak, but
the consumer still looks to be in a slightly stronger financial position than previously believed, which
helps explain recent resilience in spending and suggests consumers may have a bit more runway ahead.

Investment: Equipment, Intellectual Property Products and Inventories
• The AI build out will lead to another surge in business fixed investment spending in Q3, though the
effect on headline growth will be partly offset by stronger imports.
Business fixed investment spending continues to be supported primarily by the high-tech buildout.
We forecast real equipment investment to rise at around a 16% annualized clip in Q3, but part of
this boost to headline GDP growth will be offset by stronger import growth that is facilitating the

build out. Net imports of computers are tracking to surge in the third quarter, which will provide a
sizable lift to information processing equipment investment, or around half of overall equipment.
And while non-defense capital goods shipments (including aircraft) slipped in August, the near-term
growth trajectory remains supportive of a pickup in equipment. The rebuild in inventories we have
been anticipating is also tracking to be a bit stronger as businesses replenish drawn down product.

Investment: Residential
• The abrupt rise in mortgage rates is likely to dampen residential investment over the next few
quarters. Residential spending should find support next year as longer-term interest rates
moderate somewhat.
We are unlikely to see another positive residential investment reading in Q3. Rising mortgage rates

have sent a chill over builder outlooks, pushing sales expectations to their weakest level in more than
three years. While builders have responded with deeper price cuts, affordability pressures continue
to weigh on single-family sales and construction outlays. Apartment construction is simultaneously
soft amid rising financing costs and modest rent growth, and home improvement spending remains
constrained by high materials costs and interest rates. While residential investment is likely to remain

4 | Economics

### Page 5

Economic Outlook: October 2026 Economics
a drag this year, we expect a slight moderation in longer-term interest rates in 2027, which would
provide a more supportive backdrop.
Investment: Nonresidential Structures
• We have not made material changes to our nonresidential structures forecast. Despite elevated
interest rates, data center and related spending should continue to support structures investment
through our forecast horizon.
Nonresidential structures spending has accelerated sharply, overwhelmingly driven by data centers

and electric power facilities. As data center construction expands geographically and demand rises
for semiconductors, high-tech capex has also started to generate positive spillovers for warehousing
and manufacturing construction. This buildout, which appears relatively insensitive to interest rates,
should be enough to sustain solid growth in structures investment even as spending across most
other categories remains essentially flat. Domestic energy is another green shoot, with elevated prices

supporting ongoing growth in active rotary rigs.

Net Exports & Trade
• An expected drag from trade in Q3 got larger in light of a near-record trade deficit in August, net
exports is now expected to lop off three full percentage points from third quarter growth.

Exports increased in August for the first time in four months, but the increase was more than offset by
an even larger increase in imports. The upshot was a widening in the trade deficit to a near-record gap
of more than $105 billion. Going into the report, we had a 1.9 percentage point drag from net exports
penciled in for Q3 GDP; we revised that to a full 3.0 percentage points drag in light of the record gap.
Even a modest narrowing in the final months of the year would likely mean a lift to headline GDP in

Q4, but the real story here is that trade will remain a headwind throughout the forecast period owing
largely to surging imports of tech-related products amid the AI boom.
Labor Market
• The jobs market remains stable. We continue to expect the unemployment rate to end the year at
4.2% and hold near that level in 2027.
We modestly revised down our near-term payroll growth forecast following the September jobs
report, but otherwise we have left our labor market outlook unchanged. Measures of labor demand

growth continue to point to a stable labor market. Job openings have inched higher on a year-ago
basis but remain broadly steady while initial jobless claims, WARN notices and Challenger job cut
announcements continue to signal low layoffs. Labor force participation appears to be improving after
its sharp pullback in the first half of the year. Looking ahead, we expect payroll growth to average
around 60K per month in 2027, roughly in line with our estimate of breakeven job growth. That should

Economics | 5

### Page 6

Monthly Economics
keep the unemployment rate near 4.2% in 2027 and leave labor market conditions broadly consistent
with "full employment." Notably, our expectation for labor cost growth of ~3.4% is the reason we
continue to reiterate that labor cost growth is not a contributor to the excess inflation seen in the
data.
Inflation
• We expect the core PCE deflator to rise 3.2% and 2.6% Q4/Q4 in 2026 and 2027, respectively.
Headline inflation remains heavily influenced by developments in the Middle East, with higher oil

prices lifting gasoline and diesel prices. Food inflation has been tame this year but looks poised to
strengthen as higher transportation and commodity prices work their way through supply chains.
Beyond food and energy, price pressures remain concentrated in a handful of categories, most notably
airfares and AI-related tech goods. Core goods inflation is showing signs of stabilizing, as waning tariff-
related price pressures have been offset by resilient demand tied to the AI buildout. We expect some

improvement here in 2027. Still-low shelter inflation and cooler price growth for airfares and health
care should help bring about some moderation in core services inflation next year. We expect the core
PCE deflator to rise 3.2% on a Q4-over-Q4 basis in 2026 before slowing to 2.6% in 2027.

Fiscal Policy
• Fiscal year (FY) 2026 ended on September 30, and the federal government tallied a $2.0 trillion
budget deficit (6.5% of GDP). We project the deficit to be unchanged in dollar terms in FY 2027

and a bit smaller as a share of GDP (~6%).
• Another continuing resolution to extend government funding past the December 11 deadline
seems like the most likely outcome at this time for the FY 2027 budget. It is possible Republicans
use the lame duck session of Congress to increase defense spending and raise the debt ceiling.
Looking ahead to the lame duck session of Congress that will follow the midterm elections, Congress
needs to pass a bill that extends government funding past December 11 to avert a government

shutdown. Another continuing resolution that punts the issue into Q1 seems like the most likely
outcome. Perhaps more interesting will be whether Republicans attempt to use budget reconciliation
to pass one more partisan piece of legislation. If they do, we suspect the two things at the top the
agenda will be more funding for national defense and immigration enforcement priorities as well as a
debt ceiling increase. For the latter, raising it in the lame duck would remove some leverage Democrats

could have in 2027 if they recapture one or both chambers. It is still very early, but we estimate the
debt ceiling "X date" by which Congress must act or face default is sometime in late Q3/early Q4 of
next year.
6 | Economics

### Page 7

Economic Outlook: October 2026 Economics
Monetary Policy & Interest Rates
• Our base case forecast is for the FOMC to hike the fed funds rate by 25 bps in December and
then keep the policy rate unchanged in 2027 at 4.00%-4.25%, with risks skewed toward additional
tightening.
As we go to print, the FOMC seems more likely than not to keep the fed funds rate unchanged in
October. But given the rationale under which the Fed started this tightening cycle and the forcefulness

with which Warsh has driven home his displeasure with elevated inflation, it seems entirely plausible
to us that October will be a live meeting. At a minimum, the recent inflation data seem to make a hike
in December a lock. Past December, the outlook will be highly dependent on future inflation data. Our
expectation is that core inflation will cool gradually in 2027, and we still do not see signs of demand-
side inflation in the data. But hiking cycles have an inertial component to them, and the recent inflation

data reset the clock to zero on the Fed seeing several good inflation prints in a row. This creates upside
risk to our base case for the fed funds rate.

International Developments
• Global growth remains resilient, leaving our 2026 and 2027 forecasts unchanged. Our inflation
forecasts are also unchanged, although risks of delayed price pass-through could prolong inflation
pressures into 2027.

• Monetary policy continues to lean toward tightening across advanced economies, while emerging
market central banks are increasingly cautious, either holding rates or easing more gradually as
inflation pressures rebuild.
The balance of risks to global inflation remains tilted to the upside, complicating the monetary policy
outlook even as growth has proven more resilient than expected. Elevated energy prices, particularly
for refined petroleum products, persistent geopolitical uncertainty and El Niño-related food price

risks could prolong inflationary pressures into 2027. While second-round effects still remain relatively
contained, repeated supply shocks are testing central banks’ willingness to look through the temporary
price increases, particularly where inflation expectations remain elevated, even as higher government
bond yields are already contributing to tighter financial conditions.
Across advanced economies, central banks face increasingly difficult trade-offs as persistent inflation

pressures limit the scope for policy accommodation. The Eurozone remains particularly vulnerable
to energy supply disruptions, with elevated natural gas prices and low storage levels adding to
inflation risks. We continue to expect a 25 bps European Central Bank hike in December, followed by a
prolonged hold through 2027. Fiscal concerns are also adding to pressure, particularly in France, where
the selloff in government debt has pushed yields higher. In the U.K., resilient activity and renewed

Economics | 7

### Page 8

Monthly Economics
inflation pressures similarly underpin our expectation for a Bank of England hike in November,
followed by a hold through 2027.
Elsewhere, policy paths are becoming more differentiated. After four hikes this year brought the Cash
Rate to 4.60%, we expect the Reserve Bank of Australia to remain on hold, although renewed inflation
pressures or stronger-than-expected activity could prompt further tightening. We also still expect
the Bank of Canada to hold at 2.25%, with subdued growth weighing against energy-driven inflation

risks, although incoming inflation data over the coming weeks will be critical in determining whether
persistent price pressures warrant tightening monetary policy perhaps as early as the December
meeting. Japan remains an exception to the broader hold outlook: while the yen's recovery provides
some relief from imported inflation, elevated energy prices and import dependence continue to pose
upside risks. We expect the Bank of Japan to hike again by year-end and now expect an additional 25

bps hike in Q3-2027, bringing the terminal rate to 1.75%.
The emerging market story has increasingly shifted from easing toward patience or selective
tightening. In Asia, China's weak domestic demand and persistent property-sector strains continue to
warrant policy support, with AI-related exports providing some offset. We expect a more gradual pace
of Reserve Requirement Ratio cuts alongside targeted monetary and fiscal measures. India faces the

opposite challenge, with elevated food and energy prices and rupee depreciation strengthening the
case for further Reserve Bank of India tightening. In Latin America, peso depreciation and elevated
energy costs pose upside inflation risks for Mexico, although limited exchange-rate pass-through
should allow Banxico to remain on hold while policymakers monitor underlying inflation and inflation
expectations. In Brazil, election-related uncertainty and renewed inflation pressures are likely to delay

further easing, with cuts expected to resume in Q1-2027 once policymakers see more convincing
evidence of disinflation.

## Document 330: European resilience, American vigour (2026-10-30)

Source PDF: all report pdf/European resilience, American vigour (2026-10-30).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Paris, 30 October 2026

EcoFlash

European resilience, American vigour

Eurozone growth continues to prove resilient, notwithstanding the volatility of the Irish figures. The Q3 data reinforce 
our forecast of 1.1% growth this year, which could even be slightly exceeded, as German and Spanish momentum shows 
no sign of fading. In France, the negative surprise (-0.1% q/q against a consensus of +0.1% q/q) stems from a new one-
off factor (the sharp fall in aerospace output caused by delays in input deliveries) and masks an encouraging rebound 
in household consumption. In the United States, growth reached its highest level since Q2 2025 (0.8% q/q), driven by 
buoyant private domestic demand.

Eurozone: The resilience of growth is confirmed 
Eurozone GDP grew by 0.4% q/q in Q3 according to Eurostat's preliminary flash estimate, above both the consensus and our 
Nowcast (0.3%). The ever-volatile Irish figures contributed 0.2 pp to this outcome. Excluding Ireland, growth (0.2% q/q) 
nonetheless remains in line with the pace of recent quarters (0.2-0.3% q/q on average). This illustrates the Eurozone's capacity 
to withstand the current shocks, thanks in particular to the strength of Spain (+0.07 pp contribution to the q/q change in Eurozone’s 
GDP) and the Netherlands (+0.04 pp), and to Germany's gradual recovery (+0.06 pp). France (-0.03 pp) and Italy (zero growth), 
by contrast, posted disappointing results, although in France's case these partly reflect temporary factors (aerospace output). The 
growth carry-over for 2026 now stands at 1.0%, which supports our forecast of 1.1% in annual average terms and even points to 
upside risk. The European Commission's October surveys remain upbeat, with a further improvement in industrial activity 
expectations. 
The German recovery continues. As anticipated by our Nowcast, real GDP grew by 0.2% q/q in Q3 (after 0.3% in Q2). 
According to Destatis, consumption (private and public) increased slightly, while investment edged down. Export momentum 
slowed markedly compared with Q2. This is Germany's fourth consecutive quarter of growth. 
Italian GDP surprised on the downside, stagnating in Q3 (against a consensus of 0.1%). According to Istat, this reflects a 
decline in value added in industry, offset by increases in services and agriculture. The positive contribution from domestic demand 
was reportedly cancelled out by a negative contribution from net trade. 
Spanish growth remained robust in Q3, in line with expectations (0.6% q/q, after 0.7% in Q2), driven mainly by domestic 
demand (0.5 pp contribution) and, to a lesser extent, by net exports (0.1 pp). Consumption remains the main engine of growth 
(+0.6% q/q for private consumption and +0.9% for public consumption). Investment rose by 0.7%. Export growth (1.3%) once 
again outpaced import growth (1.1%) this quarter. All sectors except construction recorded positive changes in value added, with 
industry and services both up 0.7%. 
France: A sharp drop in aerospace output prevents a return to growth in Q3 
GDP contracted by 0.1% q/q in Q3 (after stagnating in Q2), weighed down by a sharp fall in aerospace output (-4.5% q/q 
in the production of other transport equipment, which includes aerospace). This steep decline dragged down goods production 
(down 1.2% q/q) and explains the sharp contraction in exports (-1.7% q/q) and business investment (-0.4% q/q). Net trade thus 
subtracted 0.6 pp from GDP (imports were flat over the same period). Conversely, household consumption surprised on the 
upside (+0.4% q/q), boosted by purchases of electric vehicles (registrations up 112% y/y in Q3). Public consumption 
(+0.3% q/q) also supported growth, as did activity in market services (+0.3% q/q). Despite the sharp drop in aerospace 
output in Q3, Airbus has confirmed its delivery targets for the year (with engine supply problems expected to be resolved), pointing 
to a strong rebound in growth in Q4 (0.3% q/q according to our forecasts). Overall, growth is expected to reach 0.3% in 2026, 
before picking up to 0.8% in 2027. 
United States: Growth at a one-year high  
GDP growth picked up in Q3 to 3.4% at a seasonally adjusted annualised rate (AR; i.e. 0.8% q/q), from 2.2% in Q2 
(0.6% q/q). This is the fastest pace since Q3 2025, confirming that activity is almost immune to the energy shock. Private domestic 
demand remains particularly strong. Private non-residential investment accelerated to 12.7% AR, a performance unmatched since 
the post-Covid reopening. AI-related components are driving this figure (18.5% AR), but other types of investment are also rising 
for a second consecutive quarter (3.8% AR) after contracting uninterruptedly throughout 2025. Household consumption, steady 
at 2.5% AR in real terms, is growing faster than it did before inflation picked up again, but at the cost of a significant drop in the 
saving rate (3.8% at the end of the quarter, down from 4.4% at the end of the previous quarter). The United States nonetheless 
continues to "import its growth": net trade is weighing on GDP owing to a surge in imports (19.7% AR), driven by AI-related 
components – IT, semiconductors and telecommunications (103.8% AR).

## Document 349: How the blue wave could reshape US energy and data centres

Source PDF: all report pdf/How the blue wave could reshape US energy and data centres.pdf

### Page 1

Energy aﬀordability shaped the midterms. A divided
government will now face pressure to shield consumers from
rising data centre power costs while speeding up new energy
supply. Onshoring and trade protections will continue, despite
higher near-term costs, as energy security remains a priority
Coco Zhang
After the midterm elections, the government will need to minimise data
centre-related costs for consumers, bring new power supply online faster and

protect supply chains. Pictured: Data centre in Ohio
Exit polls from the US midterm elections highlighted a key concern
shaping voters’ decisions: energy aﬀordability. Nearly 90% of
voters said the cost of living was a crucial or major factor in their
vote for Congress, with many pointing to gasoline and utility costs.
Meanwhile, as the AI data centre boom strains power supply and
infrastructure, 56% said the issue was a crucial or major factor in

their vote.
With Democrats now set to control both the House and Senate,
what does this mean for US energy and data centre policy?

### Page 2

Newly elected lawmakers will face pressure to address these
concerns, requiring the Democratic Congress and Republican White
House to ﬁnd common ground on shielding consumers from data
centre-related costs and accelerating new power supply, including
through permitting reform. Onshoring and supply chain protections
are also likely to continue. While they can raise near-term costs,
they remain important to US energy security. Developments in all
three areas could have signiﬁcant implications for businesses.

That said, Congress needs to strike a balance when drafting
legislation aimed at achieving these goals. More aggressive
measures could face a veto from President Trump.
Strengthened data centre regulation ahead,
but the devil is in the detail
The data centre industry is facing heightened local opposition.
Recent polls suggest that around one in seven Americans is
concerned about or opposes data centre development in their
neighbourhoods.
In response to this issue, there is likely to be tighter data centre

regulation. State-level policy will be more important to watch, as
action is politically and practically easier at this level. The most
likely approach is targeted regulation, much of which is already
emerging. This includes data centre-related taxes, reduced tax
incentives, environmental standards, and requirements for
developers to bear relevant cost increases. Outright moratoriums
remain unlikely to become mainstream. Where they are introduced,

they are more likely to be temporary, giving states time to develop
a data centre policy framework.
At the federal level, the key unanswered question is how much
authority should remain with the states and whether federal
legislation should mandate state regulations or simply provide
guidance. Republicans generally favour letting states lead, while
Democrats tend to support a stronger federal role.
We think sweeping data centre legislation is unlikely because of

President Trump’s veto power. Federal action is more likely to focus

on aﬀordability by protecting electricity customers from higher

More broadly on AI, we expect the Democratic Party to increase

fundamentally alter the industry’s growth trajectory. AI’s
importance to US economic growth and competition with China will
remain powerful drivers of expansion.
Business implications
State-level policy would more directly aﬀect project siting,
which is important for data centre companies to watch.
Where state rules are tighter, project development could slow.

However, a more restrictive regulatory environment could
also increase the value of data centres already approved or
under construction, easing some of the industry’s capital
constraints. Higher valuations could also encourage
developers to be more selective and expand at a more
measured pace, ultimately improving the quality of the
project pipeline.
Developers should also treat community engagement as a
strategic investment. Recent surveys show that while only

20-30% of Americans support local data centre construction,
support can rise to 50-70% when there are accompanying
community investments, such as in public infrastructure
improvements or reductions in residents' costs. More
developers are now signing binding community beneﬁt
agreements as part of project development.

Enabling faster power generation through

Both parties support timely expansion of electricity supply to meet

rising demand from AI and manufacturing reshoring. They also
generally agree that energy abundance can help reduce cost

capture, and biofuels.

speciﬁc technologies, but in permitting reform.

With both parties recognising the need for reform, the Senate
introduced the Bipartisan American Aﬀordability and Jobs Act
(BAAJA) in September 2026. Key permitting proposals include:

Speeding up approvals for energy projects
Providing greater certainty and consistency across
administrations
Making it easier to permit power transmission lines
Despite debate over its environmental impact, there is broad
agreement that the BAAJA would meaningfully improve the
permitting process. The key question is whether it can become law
under the current Congress. The Senate is expected to vote on 9
November, after which the bill would still need approval from the

House and President Trump. Democrats may want to delay the bill
and seek changes once they regain control of Congress next year,
but doing so could increase the risk of a presidential veto. This
creates an incentive to reach a deal sooner rather than later.

Even if the BAAJA does not pass under the current Congress,

Business implications
For energy companies – both renewables and fossil fuels –
the BAAJA could signiﬁcantly shorten permitting timelines. If
enacted, it would give project developers greater certainty,

reducing the risk of litigation and permit cancellations. This
could further expand project siting options for developers.
Nevertheless, in the very near term, projects may need to
retain ﬂexibility around their permitting strategy while the
new policy framework is developed and implemented.
Expect more protectionism and reshoring
efforts against China dominance
From clean energy technologies to the critical minerals that
underpin much of the US economy, China controls a large share of

global production capacity. Consequently, reducing reliance on
China will remain a US policy priority. Such eﬀorts would increase
rather than reduce project costs in the near term. But the long-term
beneﬁts of stronger domestic manufacturing and more secure
supply chains could outweigh those costs.

This creates two key risks. First, heavy reliance on China leaves US
industries vulnerable to geopolitical tensions. We have already seen
this with rare earths, where Chinese export restrictions contributed
to disruptions for US auto manufacturers. Second, China’s supply
chain dominance can put US manufacturers at a competitive
disadvantage.
Building more secure energy and natural resource supply chains

has long attracted bipartisan support in the US. The Trump
administration has supported domestic rare earth production
through funding, purchase agreements, and equity investments. In
solar, China-focused trade and tax credit restrictions are also
supporting the expansion of domestic manufacturing.
We expect this focus to continue. The executive branch could
pursue further protective measures and is unlikely to face major
opposition from Congress. Congress could also authorise additional

funding to strengthen rare earth supply chains.
Furthermore, a Democratic-controlled Congress could push to
extend clean manufacturing tax credits as part of broader
negotiations with President Trump. Given the administration’s focus
on countering China and expanding US manufacturing, an
extension could emerge as a compromise in a broader legislative
package under an optimistic scenario. All these eﬀorts would also
extend into future administrations.

Business implications
For US companies:
Clean energy companies and other industries that rely on
rare earths, including semiconductors, AI, defence, and

electronics, should have enough policy certainty to build
long-term supplier relationships outside China. In the near
term, however, diversiﬁcation could raise supply chain costs

face particular cost pressures, as some tariﬀs on supply
chain equipment also apply to non-Chinese suppliers.
For overseas companies:
US eﬀorts to build supply chains outside China are creating
opportunities for manufacturers elsewhere. The US is

supporting rare earth production in Australia and Brazil, while
solar and battery suppliers in countries such as South Korea
and Japan could beneﬁt from stronger demand. This could
also encourage more manufacturing investment in the US.
Meanwhile, European clean energy developers could face
greater competition for non-Chinese supplies. While the EU
has not imposed US-style tariﬀs, it is tightening restrictions
on Chinese equipment through funding, procurement,

auction, and subsidy rules. This could intensify global
competition for non-Chinese suppliers and push costs higher.

## Document 377: OPECプラスは増産見送りを継続、2027年の生産枠設定が次の焦点 ～足元の供給不足と将来的な供給過剰の双方をにらむ難しい舵取りが続く～

Source PDF: all report pdf/OPECプラスは増産見送りを継続、2027年の生産枠設定が次の焦点 ～足元の供給不足と将来的な供給過剰の双方をにらむ難しい舵取りが続く～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-11-02
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

World Trends

2026 年 11 月 2 日

1/4 
OPEC プラスは増産見送りを継続、2027 年の生産枠設定が次の焦点 
～足元の供給不足と将来的な供給過剰の双方をにらむ難しい舵取りが続く～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  西濵 徹 
（℡：050-5474-7495）

（要旨） 
○ OPEC プラス有志７ヵ国は 11 月 1 日のオンライン会合で、12 月の原油生産目標を据え置き、
３ヵ月連続で増産を見送った。７ヵ国合計の生産目標は日量約 3,101 万バレルである。 
○ 据え置きの背景には、イラン情勢の緊迫化がある。ホルムズ海峡などで原油輸送に支障が生
じており、目標を引き上げても実際の供給増にはつながりにくい。ＩＥＡによれば、９月の
湾岸産油国の原油輸出量は日量 1,390 万バレルと、前月から増えたものの緊迫化前の約４分
の３にとどまる。石油製品やＬＰＧの輸出は戦前の半分以下にとどまるほか、10 月には中東
のエネルギー関連施設や船舶への攻撃も再び強まった。 
○ 供給制約により原油価格は高水準で推移し、運賃や保険料の上昇も調達コストを押し上げて
いる。一方、高値の長期化は世界経済を下押しし、需要減退を招く懸念がある。ＩＥＡは 2026
年の世界石油需要を前年比で日量260 万バレル減と見込んでいる。 
○ 今後の焦点は、11 月 29 日に予定される OPEC プラス閣僚会合での 2027 年の生産枠の協議と
なる。日量約 200 万バレルの協調減産の扱いや、新たな生産基準の設定が論点だが、イラン
情勢が評価作業を複雑にしている。ＩＥＡは 2027 年に供給が日量 770 万バレル増える一方、
需要の回復は同 200 万バレルにとどまると予想しており、供給回復後には需給が緩む可能性
がある。ただし、OPEC とＩＥＡの需要見通しには隔たりがあり、先行きは不透明である。 
○ 日本など輸入国にとっても、原油高の長期化は貿易収支の悪化やインフレ圧力の増幅につな
がる。当面は、OPEC プラスの会合内容、ホルムズ海峡の航行状況、湾岸産油国の生産・輸出
動向、世界の石油需要を注視する必要がある。

【OPECプラス有志７ヵ国は 12 月の生産目標を据え置き、３ヵ月連続で増産を見送る決定】 
OPEC（石油輸出国機構）にロシアなどの主要産油国を加えた OPEC プラスの有志７ヵ国（サウジ
アラビア、ロシア、イラク、クウェート、カザフスタン、アルジェリア、オマーン）は、11 月１日
にオンライン形式で会合を開催し、12 月の原油生産目標を 11 月から据え置くことを決定した。こ
れにより、３ヵ月連続で増産が見送られることとなった。７ヵ国合計の生産目標は日量約3,101 万

### Page 2

2/4 
バレルとなり、９月以来の水準を維持する。今回の声明では、世界の原油市場の動向や先行きを検
討したうえで生産目標の据え置きを決定したと説明するとともに、各国が協調減産の枠組みを順守
する姿勢をあらためて確認した。

【イラン情勢の緊迫化に伴う供給制約を背景に、生産目標と実際の生産量の乖離が続いている】 
OPEC プラス有志国は、2023 年４月に発表し、翌５月から実施した日量 165 万バレルの自主減産に
ついて、段階的な縮小を進めてきた。2026 年９月には、この自主減産の縮小を完了したものの、そ
の後は 10 月、11 月と生産目標を据え置いており、今回も同様の判断を下した。背景には、イラン
情勢の緊迫化を受けて中東地域からの
原油輸送に支障が生じており、各国が生
産目標を引き上げても、実際の供給拡大
につながりにくい状況が続いているこ
とがある（図１）。特に、世界有数の原油
輸送の要衝であるホルムズ海峡では、船
舶の航行を巡る安全上の懸念が残り、原
油輸送に制約が生じている。湾岸産油国
は代替輸送ルートの活用などによって
輸出の回復を図っているものの、多くの
国の原油生産量は依然として生産目標
を下回っている。こうした状況では、生産目標を引き上げても実際の供給拡大につながる効果は限
定的であり、有志国は現行の生産目標を維持することが妥当と判断したと考えられる。 
実際、ＩＥＡ（国際エネルギー機関）が公表した最新の石油市場月報によれば、９月の湾岸産油
国の原油輸出量は日量1,390 万バレルと、前月から同 440 万バレル増加したものの、イラン情勢が
緊迫化する以前の水準の４分の３程度にとどまる。さらに、原油輸出は回復する一方、石油製品や
ＬＰＧ（液化石油ガス）の輸出量は依然として戦前の半分以下にとどまり、供給制約の解消には至
っていない。10 月には中東地域のエネルギー関連施設や船舶を対象とする攻撃が再び強まり、輸送
を巡る不確実性も高まっている。このため、OPEC プラスが生産目標を引き上げても、それが実際の
原油供給量の増加に結び付くとは限らず、足元の供給不足を解消するうえでは、イラン情勢の安定
化と原油輸送の正常化が不可欠といえる。

【原油市場では供給制約が価格を下支えする一方、高値の長期化による需要減退も懸念】 
こうした供給制約は、国際原油市況を
押し上げている（図２）。足元の原油価格
は、イラン情勢が緊迫化する以前の水準
を大きく上回るなど、原油市場では供給
不足への警戒が根強く残っている。さら

り、原油や石油製品の調達コストを押し
上げる要因となっている。こうした状況
の下では、仮に OPEC プラスが増産に踏み
切ったとしても、供給制約が残る限り原
油価格の大幅な下落にはつながりにくいと考えられる。 
一方、原油価格の高止まりが世界経済の下押し圧力となることも懸念される。原油高に伴うエネ
ルギー価格の上昇は、家計の実質購買力を低下させるとともに、企業の生産・輸送コストを押し上
げることで、個人消費や設備投資などの重しとなる。IEA は、最新の石油市場月報において、2026
年の世界の石油需要が前年比で日量 260 万バレル減少するとの見通しを示しており、供給制約に伴
う石油製品の供給不足や価格高騰などが需要を下押しすると見込んでいる。このように需要の減退
が進めば、将来的には原油需給が緩和する可能性もある。そのため、OPEC プラスにとっては、足元
の供給制約への対応だけでなく、原油高が世界経済や石油需要に及ぼす影響にも配慮した政策運営
が求められる。

【今後の焦点は2027 年の生産枠設定、供給回復に伴う需給緩和の可能性も課題】 
先行きについては、OPEC プラスが11 月29 日に予定する次回会合での協議が注目される。今回の
有志７ヵ国による会合とは異なり、次回は OPEC プラス全体の閣僚会合が予定されており、2027 年
の生産枠を巡る協議が主要な焦点になるとみられる。OPEC プラスは、2023 年４月の自主減産につ
いては９月に縮小を完了したものの、別途実施している日量約 200 万バレルの協調減産措置は 2026
年末まで維持している。2027 年以降の政策運営を巡っては、この減産措置の取り扱いが重要な論点
となる。さらに、加盟各国の持続可能な最大生産能力に関する評価を踏まえ、新たな生産基準を設
定する方針であるものの、イラン情勢の悪化に伴う生産設備や生産能力の拡張計画への影響などが
評価作業を複雑化させている。 
その意味では、今後の OPEC プラスの政策運営は一段と難しさを増すと予想される。イラン情勢
の緊迫化が続き、原油輸送の制約が長期化すれば、世界的な供給不足が続くことで原油価格には上
昇圧力が掛かりやすい。一方で、停戦協議の進展などをきっかけに原油輸送が正常化すれば、これ
まで供給が制約されてきた原油が市場に流入することにより、需給環境が一転して緩和する可能性
がある。ＩＥＡは2027 年の世界の石油供給量が前年比で日量770 万バレル増加すると見込む一方、

経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。
4/4
需要の回復幅は同 200 万バレルにとどまると予想しており、供給回復の進展に伴う需給緩和の可能
性を示唆している。もっとも、OPEC（日量１億 830 万バレル）とＩＥＡ（同１億 440 万バレル）の
需要見通しの間には大きな隔たりがあり、需給の先行きには不確実性が残る。したがって、OPEC プ
ラスにとっては、供給不足が続く現状と、将来的な供給過剰の双方をにらみながら、加盟国間の利
害を調整していく必要がある。
日本をはじめとする原油輸入国にとっても、原油需給を巡る不確実性は景気や物価の先行きを左
右する重要な要因となる。原油価格の高止まりは、輸入額の増加を通じて貿易収支に下押し圧力を
もたらすほか、エネルギー価格や物流コストの上昇を通じてインフレ圧力を増幅させる懸念がある。

一方、原油輸送の正常化によって供給量が回復すれば、原油価格の下落を通じてインフレ圧力が和
らぐことも期待される。ただし、当面は中東情勢を巡る不透明感が根強く、原油価格は地政学的な
動向に左右されやすい状況が続くと考えられる。今後は、次回の OPEC プラス閣僚会合での協議内
容に加え、ホルムズ海峡の航行状況、湾岸産油国の実際の生産・輸出動向、世界の石油需要の変化
などを注意深く見極める必要がある。

## Document 418: Tracking Europe’s AI transition_ A new STOXX 600 earnings-call framework (2026-11-11)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Tracking Europe’s AI transition_ A new STOXX 600 earnings-call framework (2026-11-11).pdf
Report source: DB Research
Date: 2026-11-11
Pages: 16
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Deutsche Bank 
Research Institute

Tracking Europe’s AI transition:  
A new STOXX 600 earnings-call 
framework

1 
Authors 
Michael Kirker 
Economist

November 11, 2026 
How quickly is AI moving from experimentation into everyday business, and 
where are companies reporting tangible gains? The answers matter for 
institutional investors and policymakers assessing productivity, operating 
efficiency and labour demand. Yet comparable evidence across Europe remains 
limited, making it difficult to track the pace and scale of the transition. 1 
We address this gap with a proprietary framework that turns what is said on 
STOXX 600 earnings call into three structured indicators: AI adoption 
intensity, reported productivity benefits, and the impact on workforces. 
Large language models analyse what management teams say about AI’s 
operational role, applying a common scoring framework across companies, 
sectors and countries. Our new approach provides a timely pan-European 
comparable tracker that captures not just whether AI is being mentioned, but its 
real-world impact as the adoption of the new technology unfolds. 
Key Takeaways: 
— Adoption is broadening, with signs of faster progress. AI-use scores rose 
gradually from a low base before strengthening over the past 12 months. The 
increase extends across sectors, although the pace and level of adoption 
remain uneven. 
— Workforce impacts remain limited in corporate disclosures. The 
framework does not show a broad negative workforce signal attributable to 
AI. The aggregate score remains close to the level of no impact on the labour 
force across all of our sample. However, this does not rule out AI displacing 
workers at a future date. 
— Geographic differences persist within the listed-company sample. Since 
2022, German companies in the STOXX 600 have generally recorded above-
average AI-use and reported productivity scores. Dutch and Spanish 
companies in the STOXX 600 have tended to score lower on AI use, although 
Spain has recently gone through a rapid catch up. 
— The technology J-Curve: The findings are consistent with an early stage of 
the AI transition. Companies report increasing adoption of the new 
technology, but transformational productivity gains and extensive workforce 
restructuring remain uncommon.

1 1 Our US team regularly publish on data from the USCB showing the impact of 
AI on businesses, and our German team have recently begun their own 
chartbook. In the UK, the Office for National Statistics publishes on the topic too.

### Page 2

Deutsche Bank AG Page 2

Methodology 
Sample period: Our sample begins in January 2022, providing a baseline before 
ChatGPT’s launch on 30 November 2022. AI in this framework covers both 
established and emerging technologies, so the launch of ChatGPT is a reference 
point rather than the start of all corporate AI adoption. 
Timing of observations: STOXX 600 companies report on different financial 
calendars and at quarterly or semi-annual intervals. Their earnings calls also mix 
discussion of completed activity with forward-looking guidance. 
We assign each transcript to the calendar quarter in which the earnings call takes 
place, regardless of the financial reporting period discussed. The resulting 
indicators therefore track when information about AI use and its operational 
impacts becomes public rather than when implementation or the reported effect 
occurred. This provides a common dating convention across the sample while 
retaining differences in reporting frequency and disclosure timing.

Figure 1: Coverage of the STOXX 600 earnings calls

Source: Deutsche Bank Research. Notes: Share of the STOXX 600 is based on the current company weight in the index. Data collection for the current quarter (Q4 2026) is incomplete 
as some companies are still to report

Constructing the STOXX 600 AI corpus: We use a specialised AI transcription 
engine to process earnings calls from STOXX 600 companies, restricting the 
analysis to calls for which we obtain complete transcripts of sufficient accuracy. 
In Q3 2026, the latest full quarter, the dataset covered more than 500 companies, 
representing approximately 90% of the index’s current market capitalisation 
weights (Figure 1). In the current quarter (Q4) earnings calls are still taking place. 
Nearly 250 companies with an equivalent weight to around c.60% of the STOXX 
index having already reported. 
Historical coverage typically ranges from 300 to 500 companies and represents 
more than 80% of the index’s current market-capitalisation weights. Coverage 
peaks twice a year because some European companies report semi-annually.  
11 November 2026 
Focus Europe

### Page 3

Deutsche Bank AG Page 3

### Page 4

Deutsche Bank AG Page 4

For examples of the AI’s scoring and justifications, see the tables presented in the 
Annex at the end of this report. 
Summary statistics 
Figure 2 shows the distribution of scores across all earnings-call transcripts in the 
historical sample, without weighting for company size or reporting frequency. 
These are transcript-level distributions rather than shares of companies, and they 
combine early and recent observations. They provide context for the quarterly 
indicators: 
• AI use score: Approximately 60% of earnings call transcripts do not 
discuss AI use. Among the minority that do discuss it, most fall into the 
“Active implementation” range (a score of 4-6 out of 10). 
• AI’s productivity impact: Nearly 70% of earnings calls do not mention a 
tangible AI-related productivity benefit. Among calls that do, scores are 
generally in the medium-impact band, indicating notable operational 
improvements. Transformational gains, scored at 8 or above out of 10, 
are rarely reported. 
• AI’s labour impact: Only around 10% of earnings calls discuss AI-related 
workforce adjustments. Reported changes typically receive scores of 1–
2 in magnitude on the −5 to +5 scale, with a slight tilt towards hiring and 
upskilling rather than redundancies.

Figure 2: Raw distribution of scores across the entire sample

Source: Deutsche Bank Research. Note: Results are based on the raw number of transcripts, and are independent of firm size, and biased towards firms reporting more often.

Taken together, the distributions are consistent with an early adoption phase: 
companies discuss AI implementation more often than transformational 
productivity gains or extensive workforce restructuring. A technology J-curve 
offers one interpretation of this pattern, with investment and organisational 
adaptation preceding broader benefits.  
This J-curve pattern is strongly corroborated by external micro-data, such as the 
Banco de España’s business activity survey (EBAE) of Spanish firms, which 
11 November 2026 
Focus Europe

### Page 5

Deutsche Bank AG Page 5

similarly indicates that AI has a more immediate impact on upfront investment 
and productivity than on headcount. 
AI use: A broad-based rise, with signs of faster recent adoption 
The new AI-use indicator points to a broadening transition across STOXX 600 
companies, with early signs that adoption has accelerated recently. Scores rose 
only gradually from 2022 and 2024, but the latest observations suggest a 
stronger pace across industries and country groups.  
The rise is broad-based rather than confined to one sector. Technology has led 
much of the sample, while financials show tentative recent outperformance and 
utilities have caught up from a low starting point. Among the large country groups, 
German companies have persistently recorded above-average scores.  
AI use across European industries 
To trace sectoral dispersion, we group companies by STOXX industry 
classification and calculate a weighted average of their scores for each calendar 
quarter, using their latest STOXX 600 index weights. 2  
We focus on four sectors spanning different business models: financials, the 
largest services sector; industrials, the largest physical-assets sector; technology, 
a likely early adopter; and utilities, an infrastructure-heavy sector where adoption 
may proceed more slowly.  
Figure 3 presents the AI use score for these four key industries alongside the 
range for all industry scores (represented by the shaded area). 
• The AI use score is generally low over time but is gradually rising from 
around 1 point in 2022 to around 3 in 2026. 
• AI-use scores are rising across industries, but differences between 
sectors are widening. The common direction therefore points to 
broadening adoption rather than uniform progress: sectors can 
participate in the transition while remaining at different levels of use. 
In terms of the industries of interest: 
• Technology’s early lead has narrowed. The sector’s AI-use score was 
near the top of the industry range through to 2024 but has recently 
plateaued as the broader STOXX 600 average caught up during 2025–
26.3 
• Financials and industrials broadly track the index average. Both sectors 
have remained close to the aggregate AI-use score over history, with 
financials generally a little below average, and industrials a little above. 
But financials show tentative signs of moving above average in recent 
quarters.

2 The industries are: (1) Basic Materials, (2), Consumer Discretionary, (3), Consumer Staples, (4) Energy, (5) 
Financials, (6) Health Care, (7) Industrials, (9) Real Estate, (10) Technology, (11) Telecommunications, (12) 
Utilities. 
3 Q4 2025 saw a large increase in the AI use score for the “Technology” industry, jumping up to around 6. This 
one-off spike was driven by ASML’s announcement of a significant strategic partnership with and investment 
in Mistral AI” to 'embed AI across our entire holistic portfolio in order to increase the performance and 
productivity of our systems and the yield of our customers' processes'. Because of ASML’s large market cap 
(around 49% of our Technology category), it distorts the weighted average significantly.
